Google takes over the Wyoming data centre site Crusoe had planned

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The AI data centre developer is handing over the site a day after Google pledged $17B there.

Google has bought Crusoe’s data centre site in southeast Wyoming and will build the project itself, after pledging $17B there. Bloomberg had reported Crusoe was pushed aside after failing to sign customers. Terms were not disclosed.

Google has bought Crusoe’s data centre site in southeast Wyoming and will build the project itself, Crusoe said on Friday. Neither company disclosed the terms.

The deal comes a day after Google said it would spend $17B on its first Wyoming data centre, in Laramie County. It ends Crusoe’s early work on the site, which included choosing the location, winning government approvals and planning its power supply.

Crusoe announced the project with Blackstone-backed Tallgrass Energy in July 2025, saying it could eventually grow to 10 GW. In June, Crusoe was being pushed aside after failing to sign customers, with Google raising concerns about costs and timing, Bloomberg reported.

Crusoe said it still has more than 6 GW of capacity under contract and over 40 GW in its pipeline. It is also building a data centre campus for Google near Amarillo, Texas, and raised $3.9B at a $30.9B valuation in September. Elsewhere it has landed customers, including deals with Meta in June and a reported $13B contract with Jane Street.

Developers face a tougher climate. Several new governors elected this week have promised to rein in data centres, Bloomberg said, and voters rejected data centres in 40 of 41 local ballot measures.

Everything You Need to Know About Amazon's 2026 Black Friday and Cyber Monday Sales

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Gone are the days when holiday sales had a firm beginning and end. These days, if you're not scouring the internet weeks before the actual date of the event, you will miss out on deals. Black Friday is no exception. Amazon released some vague official plans for their sale, but there's plenty that's discounted already, many reaching or matching their lowest price ever—we know, because we use tools to verify and track their deals.

While Amazon has released some press releases announcing they will be partaking in the sale (as they do every year), they haven't officially said what dates the sale will take place. Once they do, we'll update you here.

Yes, you will need to be a Prime Member to shop the exclusive sales during Black Friday and Cyber Monday. Prime membership starts at $14.99 per month. While only you can decide if buying a yearly Prime membership is worth it for you, remember you can always sign up and then cancel your Prime membership once the sale is over: Amazon offers a free 30-day trial, so you can shop during both Black Friday and Cyber Monday before canceling without being charged. Here's how to sign up for a Prime account and all of the benefits that come with it.

As stated already, just because the sale hasn't officially started, it doesn't mean you can't find great deals. Here are some of the best early Black Friday deals:

The WH-1000XM5 were at the top of their class until they were supplanted by the XM6. They're currently discounted to $198 (originally $399.99).

The Bose SoundLink Plus hasn’t been on the market for long, but it’s already earned strong reviews—CNET labeled it “the Goldilocks of Bose Bluetooth speakers,” and it’s down to $179 (originally $269).

The all-in-one Eufy E25 robot vacuum and mop has a self-cleaning roller mop, and it is 40% off at $599.98 (originally $999.99). The roller is rinsed with clean water as it mops, then cleaned at the dock, so you don't need to spend as much time cleaning it yourself.

Amazon is selling the latest MacBook Airs at a $200 discount. That brings the 13-inch MacBook Air to $1,099.99 (down from $1,299) and the 15-inch MacBook Air to $1,299 (originally $1,499), reaching their lowest prices since the June price hikes.

The Apple Watch Ultra 4 only launched in September, and it’s already seeing an early Black Friday discount of $100, bringing it down to $699.99 (originally $799).

The other major retailers doing sales are Best Buy, Target, and Walmart. Currently, Best Buy is offering one of the largest Cyber Week sales, with new deals available every Friday. Target has some week-long deals and a Deal of the Day. Nowadays, both large retailers and small businesses compete for Cyber Monday shoppers, so you can expect practically every store to run sales through Monday, Nov. 30, 2026. The “best” sales depend on your needs, but in general, the biggest discounts tend to come from larger retailers that can afford lower prices: think places like Amazon, Walmart, Target, Best Buy, and Home Depot. You can find all the best sales from major retailers on our live blog.

If this will be your first Black Friday shopping at Amazon, there are some basic things you need to know about Amazon sales:

You can share your Prime membership with family members even if they don't live with you.

You can set price alerts for products you want and your Alexa devices can notify you when they go on sale.

There are usually some "Invite-Only" deals that you can sign up for.

There are tools you can use to see if the deal you're looking at is worthwhile or not.

But if you only take one bit of advice for shopping on Amazon, let it be this one: Don't buy anything you weren't going to buy anyway. A good way to make sure you do this is by making a list of the products you do want, and only having eyes for those.

Black Friday used to be bigger for major retailers and more expensive tech and appliances, while Cyber Monday was for cheaper tech and gave smaller businesses a chance to compete online. Nowadays, though, the distinction is almost meaningless. Every major retailer will offer sales on both days, and the smart move is to know what you want, use price trackers or refer to guides like our live blog that use price trackers for you, and don’t stress over finding the perfect timing.

Yes, Amazon has Black Friday sales, but prices aren’t always what they seem. Use a price tracker to make sure you’re getting the best deal, or refer to guides like our live blog that use price trackers for you. And if you have an Amazon Prime membership, make the most of it.

You can find all the best sales from major retailers on our Black Friday coverage.

In short, yes, Black Friday still offers discounts that you're less likely to see throughout the rest of the year. If there’s something you want to buy, or you’re shopping for gifts, it’s a good time to look for discounts on what you need, especially tech sales, home improvement supplies, and fitness tech.

Charter to Cut $2 Billion In Costs Next Year Under “New Operating Model”

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

That's not counting the $1 billion in its expected Cox synergies.

Charter Communications sees the writing on the wall. It reads: “You need to cut costs.”

Charter, which is the home to Spectrum, calls itself “America’s Internet Company” — and for good reason. Charter is the leading internet and video provider in the nation providing services to more than 70 million homes and businesses across 45 states. In August, Charter Communications combined with Communications.

Now, CEO Chris Winfrey is unveiling a major reorganization.

“With the combination of Charter and Cox, we have the scale and network capabilities to connect more Americans than ever before,” Winfrey said in a statement accompanying the company’s third-quarter earnings results.

“Our purpose is Connecting Gigabit America,” Winfrey said. “Today, we are announcing decisive steps to unlock our full potential.”

Winfrey continued, “We are fundamentally rethinking how we operate and compete, with a renewed focus on delivering the best products and services. We will return Charter to Internet customer growth and transform Charter into a simpler, faster, more agile organization — and reignite our culture to win. We have the assets and competitive advantages to succeed and are moving with urgency to deliver more value for our customers and stronger results for our investors.”

To that end, Charter is “targeting $2 billion in annualized run-rate operating expense savings over the next 18 months,” the accompanying press release read, “in addition to more than $1 billion in anticipated Cox Transactions synergies.”

The cost-cutting program “will focus on eliminating nonessential activities, simplifying organizationalstructures and driving greater efficiencies through vendor partnerships,” per Charter. “Together, these initiatives are expected to strengthen Charter’s competitive position, drive improved financial performance and support significantly higher free cash flow generation over time.”

If only the actual company earnings offered as much optimism. Charter lost $2.1 billion in Q3; the Charter side lost 156,000 internet customers; Cox lost 77,000.

Rose Lavelle Suits Up to Build Gotham FC’s Future

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Committed to the reigning NWSL champions for three more seasons, the soccer star reflects on leadership, life lessons, and a monumental move to a shiny new stadium in New York City.

Rose Lavelle is breaking down her outfit of the day, though it’s not the ensemble you’d expect from a professional athlete’s social media feed.

“My hard hat, which is… something,” the Gotham FC midfielder says with a chuckle. She looks down to survey the rest of her mandatory construction-site-chic attire. “My gloves—gardener’s. My boots—too big. My vest—cool.”

It’s a Tuesday afternoon in September, and Rose is the first of her teammates to tour the unfinished Etihad Park in Queens, New York, the stadium that Gotham will call home in 2028. Earlier that day, Gotham announced that Rose had signed a contract extension through 2029. In other words, she'll be back when this concrete floor is an actual soccer pitch.

We weave past steel beams and through what will eventually become Gotham's tunnel to the pitch. From there, the grandstands rise around us, a preview of the 25,000-seat venue that will soon welcome Gotham's fans. Rose poses for photos, holding a jersey that says “2029.”

A few days later, Gotham’s president of soccer operations, Yael Averbuch West, tells me she smiled when she saw the images of Rose in her hard hat. The look may be a departure from her usual uniform, but Averbuch West sees something fitting about it. As Gotham prepares to make another postseason run in pursuit of defending their 2025 National Women’s Soccer League title, Rose has become central to the club's plans.

“She has been part of literally constructing this vision with us,” Averbuch West says. “She has been an instrumental part of the vision of the club and our aspirations and our ambition.”

The roughly $780 million Etihad Park is being developed by Major League Soccer's New York City FC, which will share the stadium with Gotham beginning in 2028. It's a significant upgrade from Gotham's current arrangement at Sports Illustrated Stadium in Harrison, New Jersey, where the team uses the visitors' locker room and covers up the New York Red Bulls’ logos with its own banners. At Etihad Park, Gotham will have a locker room of its own, merchandise space in the team store, and digital signage and video boards that can be fully branded for Gotham on match days—no covering up another team's logos required.

The move is another sign of just how much women's professional soccer has changed since Rose entered the NWSL nearly a decade ago. And in that time, Rose has changed, too. At 31, the once up-and-coming star has become a veteran helping set the standard for a club that has no plans to slow down.

The NWSL is not the same league it was when Rose was drafted first overall by the now-defunct Boston Breakers in 2017, playing home games at a 2,500-seat facility at Harvard University. When I sit down with Rose after our stadium tour, she tells me it’s difficult to compare the things that mattered to her as a rookie to signing a contract extension now. “I think of the amount of support staff we had, it was so few—how many people were dedicated towards ticketing and marketing and all of that,” she says. “I notice that stuff a lot more now than I probably did at the time, because you don't know what you don't know.”

Rose has come a long way since then. She won the 2019 World Cup with the U.S. Women’s National Team, earned Olympic gold in Paris in 2024, and navigated injuries and moves between NWSL clubs. She joined Gotham in 2024 and, after ankle surgery sidelined her for the start of her second season, returned to help the club win the 2025 NWSL championship, scoring the winning goal. In January, she was named U.S. Soccer's 2025 Female Player of the Year.

On the field, Rose’s influence isn't always captured in goals or assists. Gotham rookie Andrea Kitahata calls her the club’s “quiet momentum-builder and tone-setter” for how she initiates the team's defensive press and creates attacking opportunities that may not show up on a stat sheet.

Rose has learned plenty from USWNT veterans like Megan Rapinoe, Becky Sauerbrunn, Alex Morgan, Christen Press, and Tobin Heath, who showed her that there was no single way to command respect in a locker room. “Everybody led in their own way,” Rose says. “Everybody's leadership style might have looked different, but it wasn't any more or less impactful than the other.”

Now, the younger players in Gotham’s locker room are learning from Rose—especially since her 2025 ankle surgery, the first of her career, which forced her to find new ways to contribute off the field.

She initially thought surgery would be a straightforward fix. “I think I thought you get surgery to fix it, and when you're off the table, you’re fixed, and it's smooth sailing. And it was not smooth sailing at all,” she says. She pauses. “Yeah, it was really painful! It was really painful.”

The physical recovery was only part of the challenge. Rose says the mental toll of rehabbing—and the uncertainty that came with it—tested her in ways she hadn't anticipated. “It taught me I had a whole ’nother level of mental grind and fortitude that I could battle through,” she says. “I think you learn the most about yourself in the tough moments. I mean, it was hard—but I made it through, and I feel like I gained so much.”

Rose has never been the loudest voice in the locker room, but recent years have taught her that she doesn’t need to be.

Ahead of the 2025 season, Rose told Averbuch West that she would be more intentional about creating the change she wanted to see within the club. “When you're Rose Lavelle, you are a leader, whether you choose to be or not,” Averbuch West says. “It doesn't have to come in big speeches in front of the group. It’s not going to be that. But it's the little things she does behind the scenes, the little connections with players—pulling someone aside, talking on the field.”

Her teammates take cues from her, Averbuch West says. If Gotham is down a goal at halftime, they look to Rose to gauge how she's responding. If Rose is inspired, they’re inspired. And Rose has become increasingly intentional about the example she sets. “She's been very proactive during her time here to share really constructive feedback with us, and to also be part of creating a locker room culture that I think the players at this point are really enjoying,” Averbuch West says.

“Obviously, we had some cheaters in there,” Kitahata jokes. “Rose made sure that she held everyone accountable, made sure no one's looking it up on their phone.”

And Rose isn’t exactly a quiet participant. “She's in the meal room screaming out,” Kitahata recalls, imitating Rose’s clues: “Blank, blank, blank, blank. It's four words. Figure it out, guys!”

She’s equally serious about her teammates’ recovery. During a July road trip to Portland, Rose took Kitahata, still navigating her first professional season, along for a day of mental and physical recovery. They got coffee, browsed the shelves at Powell's Books, and headed to a sauna and cold plunge. A stickler for her recovery routine, Rose insisted they stay submerged up to their necks in the icy water for four minutes. “I'm like, ‘Rose. Rose, I'm freezing!’” Kitahata recalls. But Rose wouldn't budge. “I love that she always holds me accountable for my recovery.” They finished the day with sushi.

Rose is the teammate you want to run into when you’re having a bad day, because she notices, Kitahata says. “Even with a look, she's like, ‘Oh my god, what's wrong? Do we need to go get coffee?’ … It’s very rare to find someone of her stardom that is just so willing to give to the team.”

Rose has become more conscious of how much she'll miss these moments someday. “I'm such a painfully nostalgic person,” she says. “It's like, debilitating sometimes, honestly.”

It's the everyday moments with her teammates that seem to stick with her most. “I was thinking the other day, having moments in the locker room where we have this shared playlist that we're playing, and we're all dancing to it. And I was like, ‘How many grown adults get to dance with their friends in the locker room?’” she says. “It’s little things like that that it's so easy to take for granted, because we get to do it literally every single day, but something that I think is just so special.”

It's also clear how much she values the life she's built at Gotham, and how much she wants to keep being part of it. “I love my life off-field. I love my life on-field. I think we have such an incredible group of players and staff that we get to work with and learn from each day,” she says. “I just felt so content, but also challenged in a way that I felt like I was going to be happily growing.”

For Gotham, the move to Queens isn't just about having a place to call its own. It's also an opportunity to reach a bigger audience. Despite winning two NWSL championships in three seasons, Gotham has struggled to fill the stands in New Jersey consistently. But when the club hosted the Washington Spirit at Citi Field, home of the New York Mets, for its Queens Classic in July, the sellout crowd of 42,175 offered a glimpse of what might be possible across the street at Etihad Park.

The move isn't without its complications. Gotham has deep roots in New Jersey, and some longtime supporters are disappointed to see the club leave. “I was a little sad at first that the team was moving,” says Lindsey Smith, 37, a New Jersey resident and five-year season ticket holder. “But this is so much bigger than a location to me. As someone who wants to make women's sports accessible, I'm very excited for Gotham to move to Queens.”

Smith’s friend Krista Tonnesen, 37, has been attending Gotham matches regularly since 2021. An Astoria resident, that has meant long trips home after night games, sometimes with 40-minute waits between PATH trains. She's thrilled to have Gotham in her borough, and hopeful that the shorter trip will bring more fans into the stands. “The players and the team deserve better,” she says. “I really think moving to New York will turn that around.”

Rose says the atmosphere at the Queens Classic, where she scored the game's only goal, left an impression on her. “To think that we could be playing in front of a crowd and atmosphere like that, every single home game, I think it'll just add that much more to what we're building,” she says.

Back at Etihad Park, as we make our way through the unfinished concrete hallways, I ask Rose if she can picture what it'll be like when Gotham finally takes the field here. “I see the vision,” she says.

She also leads the sports and athletes vertical, traveling to cover the Paris Olympics, Women’s World Cup, WNBA Finals, and NCAA Final Four for WH. She has nearly 15 years of experience writing, editing, and managing social media for national and international publications and is also a NASM-certified personal trainer. NIKKE players told to scan their PCs after hackers used the game’s launcher to spread malware

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

NIKKE players are being urged to check their Windows PCs after Level Infinite confirmed that files in the game’s official PC launcher were tampered with and used to distribute malicious programs.

In an official announcement, the publisher said an open-source domain used by some of its services was hijacked on September 27. Launcher script files were then partially modified, with the distribution of malicious programs beginning on October 30.

The issue was detected on November 4, after which Level Infinite replaced the affected files and refreshed its content delivery network. The publisher says the currently distributed version of the launcher is safe to use, but players who opened the Windows client after September 27 at 15:41 UTC+9 are being asked to perform a security check. Mobile-only players are not affected.

The updated PC client includes a security feature designed to detect and remove malicious files linked to the incident. Players are advised to close the game, launcher, and all browsers before starting the check.

【Manual Security Check Instructions for the PC Launcher】Commanders,This is the GODDESS OF VICTORY: NIKKE Operations Team.We sincerely apologize for the great concern and distress caused by the recent PC launcher security incident.If you have launched the Windows PC… https://t.co/3fzDRYJ9Kk

NIKKE’s manual instructions also tell players to inspect the launcher’s file for the phrase . If the phrase appears, the company says a malicious program may have executed on the PC.

Players are also advised to update their antivirus software, run a full system scan and perform a Windows Security Offline Scan. Level Infinite recommends changing passwords for accounts used on the affected PC, as well as revoking API keys and access tokens. Those steps should be completed from a different device or after the PC has been secured.

The full extent of the incident remains unclear. However, BleepingComputer reported that the malware included a remote-access agent capable of running commands, browsing and downloading files, capturing screenshots and collecting browser data. Analysis also found functionality linked to cryptocurrency wallet information, although it has not been confirmed whether attackers stole cryptocurrency from players.

First Infinity Nikki patch offers free rewards to all players

Alongside the security instructions, Level Infinite issued in-game compensation to Commanders. The package shown in player screenshots includes 3,000 Gems, 10 Advanced Recruit Vouchers, seven 24-hour Growth Sets, 20 Custom Modules and 100 Ultra-Double Boost Modules.

This is the compensation for literal malware btw pic.twitter.com/sbZJ06MvYz

The reward quickly drew criticism on X, with player Lilac writing: “This is the compensation for literal malware btw.”

Level Infinite said it plans to review its security management practices, strengthen monitoring for unauthorized file changes and introduce additional integrity checks for resources accessed by the launcher.

Looksmaxxing Trend Poses Health Risks for Young Men

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A clinical commentary published in the Journal of Adolescent Health warns that the viral online trend known as “looksmaxxing” exposes adolescent boys and young men to severe psychological and physical harms. Led by Dr. Kyle T. Ganson of the University of Toronto, the authors describe how attempts to optimize male facial and physical attractiveness cross from routine grooming into life-threatening behaviors, including extreme dietary restriction, unregulated peptide and steroid abuse, and self-injurious practices like “bonesmashing.”

The paper urges pediatricians, psychiatrists, and adolescent health clinicians to recognize looksmaxxing subcultures, differentiate benign hygiene from body dysmorphia, and screen for intersecting eating disorders, steroid use, and manosphere radicalization.

In recent years, the algorithmic landscape of social media platforms has given rise to “looksmaxxing”, an online subculture centered on the systematic pursuit of maximizing physical attractiveness. Rooted in anonymous web forums and popularized across TikTok, YouTube, and Discord, the trend presents male aesthetic value as an objective, quantifiable science governed by facial symmetry, prominent cheekbones, sharp mandibular angles, and muscular, low-body-fat physiques.

While early engagement may involve standard self-care, such as adopting a skincare regimen, lifting weights, or addressing androgenetic alopecia, the subculture frequently operates as an algorithmic pipeline toward radical body modification and psychological distress.

In a clinical commentary published in the Journal of Adolescent Health, adolescent health expert Dr. Kyle T. Ganson, PhD, MSW, associate professor at the University of Toronto’s Factor-Inwentash Faculty of Social Work, alongside international colleagues, issues a call to action for healthcare providers. The authors highlight that vulnerable boys and young men are increasingly turning to extreme, unregulated practices to conform to rigid physical archetypes, with severe repercussions for mental and physical health.

The researchers emphasize that looksmaxxing operates along a wide, insidious spectrum. At the lower-risk end, adolescents exchange tips on haircuts, dental hygiene, and fitness. However, the culture frequently pushes members toward “hardmaxxing”, invasive, unverified, or explicitly hazardous interventions.

“Looksmaxxing exists on a spectrum, and it is important that we do not automatically pathologize normal grooming, exercise, or appearance-related behaviours among adolescent boys and young adult men,” said Dr. Ganson. “At the same time, clinicians need to recognize when these behaviours become extreme, unsafe, or connected with significant distress and impairment.”

The rapid spread of looksmaxxing is accelerated by algorithmic echo chambers. Young men often place greater trust in charismatic social media influencers, manipulated “before-and-after” photographs, and anonymous numerical facial ratings than in qualified medical professionals.

Furthermore, looksmaxxing spaces rarely exist in a cultural vacuum. The commentary highlights how these forums frequently intersect with broader “manosphere” ideologies, incel (involuntary celibate) subcultures, and hyper-masculine content. In these spaces, physical appearance is treated as a deterministic prerequisite for romantic success, social status, and human worth. When young men inevitably fail to attain impossible physical ideals, the resulting cognitive distortions can lead to profound demoralization, severe depressive episodes, and suicidality.

Because traditional gender norms often discourage young men from admitting to body image insecurities or seeking psychiatric help, appearance-related distress in males frequently goes undetected until severe medical complications emerge.

The authors call on pediatricians, adolescent medicine specialists, dermatologists, and mental health clinicians to become fluent in looksmaxxing terminology and systematically screen young male patients presenting with obsessive fitness or grooming routines.

The commentary recommends comprehensive assessment across several key domains:

Rather than dismissing or ridiculing adolescents’ appearance concerns, which can alienate patients and push them deeper into insular online forums, the authors advocate for an empathetic, harm-reduction approach. Clinicians should validate young men’s desires for self-improvement while directly dismantling online pseudoscience, correcting biological misinformation, and warning against permanent structural injury.

Additionally, fostering digital media literacy can empower young men to deconstruct algorithmic manipulation, recognize edited imagery, and reject narrow, toxic beauty ideals.

“Boys and young men are navigating a digital environment where appearance advice, pseudoscience, commercial interests, and potentially dangerous health behaviours can become intertwined,” concluded Dr. Ganson. “Understanding that environment is increasingly important for clinicians, researchers, families, and others who support boys and young men.”

Looksmaxxing is an online subculture and trend popular among adolescent boys and young men dedicated to maximizing physical attractiveness. While it begins with common hygiene, grooming, and fitness practices, it often progresses to extreme, dangerous behaviors to achieve rigid aesthetic ideals like chiseled jawlines and hyper-muscular builds.

What are the most dangerous practices associated with looksmaxxing?

Extreme practices, often called “hardmaxxing”, include severe caloric starvation, the use of illicit anabolic steroids, peptides, and SARMs, unsupervised chemical skin whitening, and “bonesmashing” (striking one’s own facial bones with heavy objects in a misguided attempt to change jaw structure).

How should clinicians address looksmaxxing in adolescent male patients?

Clinicians should adopt a harm-reduction approach rather than dismissing appearance concerns. They should become familiar with looksmaxxing terminology, evaluate patients for body dysmorphic disorder, eating disorders, and steroid misuse, correct pseudoscientific misinformation, and address underlying loneliness, depression, or exposure to extremist online communities.

Looksmaxxing among adolescent boys and young men: increasing awareness among clinicians

Looksmaxxing is a contemporary phenomenon focused on “maximizing” physical attractiveness. Engagement with looksmaxxing exists along a spectrum, from seemingly benign grooming practices to dangerous and life-threatening behaviors; its underlying ideals are often rooted in pseudoscience, rigid appearance standards, and potentially harmful online community norms.

Clinicians must be prepared to recognize its language, assess the degree of involvement, and intervene with critical awareness and attention to co-occurring risks such as disordered eating, substance use, self-harm, and misogynistic or extremist beliefs.

Can You Upgrade To GTA 6’s $100 Edition? Yes, And It’s Easy–Here’s How

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Rockstar's Grand Theft Auto 6 launches on November 19. If you're planning to buy the $80 edition but want to upgrade later to the $100 version, you might be interested to know if such a thing is possible.

Thankfully, yes, it is-and it's very easy to do.

Rockstar will offer an "Ultimate Edition Upgrade" for $20, so for anyone who buys the $80 version and decides they do want the additional perks, they can upgrade. This applies to both the digital edition of GTA 6 that you can buy from the Xbox and PlayStation stores, as well the code-in-a-box physical edition.

The $100 Ultimate Edition, which is not sold in stores, includes a lot of extras, including vehicles, weapons, clothing for your in-game characters. The items are "threaded across all aspects of Jason and Lucia's story," Rockstar said, with new items becoming available "behind each chapter." Additionally, people who buy the $100 version get access to in-game businesses that won't be available to everyone else, including a hair salon, a tattoo parlor, a mod shop, and more.

Meanwhile, all preorders for GTA 6 come with the The Vintage Vice City Pack
and one month of GTA+.

In other news, preloading for GTA 6 begins November 10, and that's good news considering the game weighs in at a whopping 212GB. The game officially unlocks at midnight local time, so pack your bags for New Zealand if you want to play it sooner.

Ueno Bank scandal plunges Paraguay into political crisis

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Suspected financial fraud, public money at risk and millions of customers affected: Paraguay is in serious crisis following the sudden collapse of Ueno Bank. President Santiago Pena is facing impeachment.

The Ueno Bank collapse is turning into one of the biggest financial and political scandals in the history of Paraguay. At issue are suspected banking irregularities, hundreds of millions of dollars in public funds, and business ties between the bank and the country's president, Santiago Pena.

The crisis was triggered on November 3, when the Paraguayan central bank (BCP) initiated resolution procedures against Ueno after determining that it had breached minimum solvency requirements. The public prosecutor has since announced three investigations into suspected financial crimes. 27 individuals have already been formally charged.

Pressure on President Pena intensified. On November 5, he tendered his resignation, but the Paraguayan Congress did not accept it. Instead, the Chamber of Deputies voted unanimously to impeach him. A joint session of both legislative chambers was called to consider the resignation, but the session was not quorate, so no decision could be taken. Pena remains in office for now, and the Senate has opened impeachment proceedings.

Ueno Bank is a Paraguayan digital bank belonging to the Grupo Vazquez, a big Paraguayan conglomerate. Launched in 2021, it expanded remarkably fast: It recently had around three million customers, almost half the population of Paraguay. At the same time, there were warnings that the bank might have extended excessive loans to companies associated with its owners.

On November 3, 2026, the Paraguayan Central Bank (BCP) intervened, having determined that the bank had breached minimum solvency requirements: It no longer had sufficient capital to provide security for risks it had incurred. Business operations were abruptly suspended, leaving millions of customers unable to access their accounts.

Investigations are primarily focused on three potential problems.

First, Ueno Bank is believed to have extended credit to other companies from the same group, and these may have exceeded legal limits.

Some business dealings may also have served to conceal the lack of capital, and make the bank's financial situation appear better than it actually was.

There is also the question of why the Central Bank didn't intervene sooner, despite indications that there were problems.

Ueno Bank held huge deposits of public money. Its most important investors include the Instituto de Prevision Social (IPS), the Paraguayan social security agency responsible for pensions and social welfare payments. According to media reports, Ueno Bank administers public funds totalling as much as 800 to 850 million US dollars.

Not only is the size of the amount problematic, so is the concentration of public funds in a single financial institution — one that has had to be placed under state control. Other state institutions are also said to have continued to deposit funds with the bank.

This raises questions about how state institutions make their investment decisions, and the risks to which they have exposed public funds.

There is Pena's business relationship with Grupo Vazquez, to which the Ueno Bank belongs. The Paraguayan president maintained business connections with the conglomerate until March 2025, long after his election as head of state. However, he denies having had any direct involvement with the bank.

On the other hand, Pena's appointment of Carlos Carvallo as president of the Central Bank in 2023 has come under scrutiny. The decision is controversial because until then Carvallo was the chair of the finance company that became Ueno Bank, Financiero Ueno. It raises questions about possible conflict of interest, and the independence of banking supervision.

Carvallo resigned on November 3, at Pena's request.

On November 4, the public prosecutor's office formally charged 27 people: eight Ueno Bank executives, and 19 others with connections to companies associated with the bank.

The investigations are looking into suspected breach of trust, attempted fraud, breach of duty in crisis situations, and violation of the legal obligation to keep records.

On November 5, a judge remanded several former bank executives in custody, including Federico Miguel Vazquez Villasanti, the president of Grupo Vazquez. Two other accused were placed under house arrest.

The Central Bank has initiated winding-up proceedings against Ueno Bank. Deposits of sums up to the equivalent of 37,600 US dollars per customer are protected by law. A law has also been passed that will enable around 20,000 US dollars per saver to be paid out early. However, it is still not clear when the affected customers will actually get their money back, or what will happen to deposits greater than the legally protected maximum.

The political situation is more complicated. Santiago Pena tendered his resignation, but remains in office. A joint session of the two chambers of Congress could not decide whether or not to accept his resignation as the meeting was not quorate. The Senate has now initiated impeachment proceedings on charges of serious breaches of duty. Pena has announced that he will face the proceedings and exercise his right to defend himself.

What is at stake here is political immunity. If Pena leaves office in a regular way – by resigning — he will automatically become a senator for life, and this will give him parliamentary immunity from prosecution. If he is impeached, he loses this status, and can be prosecuted.

Should Pena leave office, the constitution decrees that his vice-president, Pedro Alliana, would take over as president. Congress would then have to elect a new vice-president. The current term of office runs until August 2028.

Chris Pratt Dropped A Spider-Man Meme To Support Amazon’s Action Stars. Alan Ritchson’s Reaction Is A+

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An Alan Ritchson And Arnold Schwarzenegger Buddy Flick Is The Christmas Movie I Didn't Know I Needed

Andrew Garfield Just Had A Really Morbid Take On Playing Spider-Man (But He’s Not Wrong)

Thank Goodness For Reacher Season 4, Because We All Wondered What It's Like To Film A Sex Scene With Alan Ritchson

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Of course, the Reacher star had a funny response.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I’ve always thought about The Terminal List, Reacher and Jack Ryan in very similar ways. They’re all book-to-screen adaptations of very popular novels, they all star very big and fit men as the leads, and they’re all very, very, very action-packed. Well, now Chris Pratt has called the similarities out too with a Spider-Man meme. Of course, in reaction to it, Reacher himself, Alan Ritchson, had an A+ comment.

In a year where Reacher and Terminal List both dropped new seasons on the 2026 TV schedule, and Tom Clancy’s Jack Ryan: Ghost War premiered on the 2026 movie schedule, we’ve gotten to see Alan Ritchson, Chris Pratt and John Krasinski back in action in very big ways. The Parks and Recreation actor gave a nod to that too, as he posted this funny meme to his Instagram:

A post shared by Chris Pratt (@prattprattpratt)

Ritchson, Pratt and Krasinski really do kind of feel like the Peter 1, Peter 2 and Peter 3 of the Amazon Prime action universe. I mean, not only are they all big action thriller series centered around former military men, but as the Guardians of the Galaxy star hilariously pointed out, all their characters' initials are JR – Jack Ryan, Jack Reacher and James Reece.

Jokes aside, Pratt praised Reacher and Jack Ryan in his post, telling his followers that he “HIGHLY” recommends watching both projects with an Amazon Prime subscription. However, to bring the jokes back into the mix, Alan Ritchson pointed right back at his action bros of Prime Video by commenting:

This is the first time AI hasn’t made me look like [John Cena] and Matt Damon’s love child 😂Love your show, buddy! Happy to do the Spider-Man point back at you two.

I have to say, for the most part, in this post, Ritchson does look like himself. And I am laughing at the fact that, in a way, he is kind of a mix between John Cena and Matt Damon. Seriously, you can always count on the Reacher star to have something funny and unexpected to say on social media.

It’s also lovely to see him hyping up Jack Ryan and Terminal List, just like Pratt showed love for Krasinski’s project and Reacher. All three adaptations have really had a big year, so it’s fun to see these guys applaud each other for the work they’ve done.

In terms of Pratt’s series, The Terminal List finally returned for Season 2 after nearly four years of waiting. While its spinoff, Dark Wolf, did come out between the two seasons, it was great to see James Reece back at the center of the story.

As for Jack Ryan, John Krasinski returned to the role in a movie this year, three years after the release of Season 4.

Meanwhile, Season 4 of Reacher came out a few months ago, and so did its first spinoff series, Neagley. It’s also worth noting that Season 5 of Reacher is in production, and it's already been renewed for Season 6. So, Alan Ritchson is well on his way to living out his dream of playing the character for at least ten seasons.

All around, it’s been a big year for “THE JRS,” as Chris Pratt lovingly named this ragtag team of action shows, and hopefully there’s more to come. While we wait for that, though, I’ll be thinking about this Spider-Man meme, Ritchson’s comment, and the possibility of one day seeing all these actors in one big project together.
